Another path: The previous SOTA LP update may be the key. It might be from a known algorithm for minimizing max convolution of nonnegative sequences. We can implement and test conceptually. Let's derive the LP more carefully and see if it indeed yields monotonic improvement for F=∥g*g∥? Suppose we have f and define g via LP max sum s.t. f*g≤M. Let S=sum g. Then g=S^{-1} direction. Consider new f'=(f+g)/2? Could new max ≤ M? Let's test. f' * f' = (f*f + f*g + g*f + g*g)/4. We know cross ≤M. Need f*g? max M. f*f ≤M. But g*g unknown. Not controlled. If also g is feasible for g? LP only cross. If g is extreme, maybe g*g ≤M? Not guaranteed. But maybe because g is the unique vector maximizing sum under cross bound, and f normalized, by von Neumann minimax this gives a stationary point of a zero-sum game? The Lagrangian? Maybe the update reduces F due to a property of (1-t)f+tg expansion: derivative at t=0 negative if cross bound and sum g >1. Let's compute derivative of max coefficient when moving f to g. For coefficient k, D(t)=(1-t)^2 C_k(f)+t(1-t)(C_k(f*g)+C_k(g*f))+t^2 C_k(g) = C_k(f)+t[ -2C_k(f)+C_cross(k)+? if f*g and g*f same? Check cross terms: 2t(1-t) f*g plus t^2 g*g. If f*g(k)≤M and C_k(f) maybe at max M. For active k with C_k(f)=M, derivative = -2M + cross + ... If cross ≤M, derivative ≤ -M <0 for small t (ignoring g*g t^2). So active maxima decrease initially! This is why update works for the maximum: even though g*g may be large, for small t its t^2 effect is negligible; if g*g not too huge, maybe. More generally, the directional derivative of max at active set is ∑ weights(-2M + cross_k) where cross≤M, so ≤ -M∑weights <0. Thus F decreases for sufficiently small t if g*g is finite (bounded). Thus we can take small step towards g. Nice. The LP direction g reduces active coefficients linearly. Repeating with small t can descend. So we don't need g itself feasible for square. We can use gradient-like step! The LP gives a direction with f*g≤M and sum g large; the cross coefficients are below M, active ones decrease.

Thus algorithm:
- Normalize current f (sum 1).
- M = max conv(f,f).

Let's formulate objective in code:
```
def fast_conv_max(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq,float)
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    return float(c.max())

def fast_score(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01: return inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    return 2*a.size*c.max()/s*s
```
Use normalized sum=1 for search to avoid scaling; score=2n*M.

FFT convolution:
```
def convolve_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    m=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    fa*=fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa, m)[:2*n-1]
    # due to roundoff, clip tiny negatives
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-12]=0
    return c
```
Since a nonnegative, real. For n=2535, m=8192? 2n-1=5069, next power 8192. rfft length 8192 fast. For many eval maybe still ok. Could use `scipy.fft.rfft` if faster but numpy okay.

Need implement LP fast and not fail. Let's write `solve_lp_direction(p, M, timeout)`.

Approx LP via scipy:
```
from scipy.optimize import linprog
from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix

def build_convolve_matrix(p):
    n=len(p); rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
    # For each k, valid i range max(0,k-(n-1))..min(n-1,k)
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
       if i0<=i1:
          idx=np.arange(i0,i1+1)
          vals=p[k-idx]
          rows.extend([k]*len(vals)); cols.extend(idx); data.extend(vals)
    return csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
```
Total nnz 2n(n+1)/2 ≈n^2. For n=512 ~262k, good. For n=1000 ~1M, okay. Time to build maybe. But linprog may need dense matrix? It can convert; okay. We can use `A_ub` as sparse. `linprog` with highs supports sparse.

```

tmp[i:i+n] = tmp[i:i+n] - 2*eps*p
tmp[j:j+n] = tmp[j:j+n] + 2*eps*p

```

Which moves to propose? We can prioritize source from high p and target from low p, and also consider active lags. For source i, subtract shifted p_i; for target j, add shifted p_j. The change in a particular active lag k: L_k/ε=2(p_{k-j} - p_{k-i}). To reduce c_k, want p_{k-j} < p_{k-i} (target's counterpart lower than source's counterpart). LP implementation more detailed:
```
def lp_direction(p, M, time_limit=20.0):
    n=len(p)
    # if n too large or no scipy: return uniform direction
    p_lp = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    # Normalize? p sum 1.
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i+n, i] = p_lp  # p_lp[j], row i+j
    c = -np.ones(n)
    b = M * np.ones(2*n-1)
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={...})
    if res.success and res.x is not None:
       g = np.clip(res.x,0,None)
       S = g.sum()
       if S>1+1e-6:
           # make feasible due to tolerance:
           cross = convolve_fast(p_lp, g)
           if cross.size and cross.max()>M:
               scale = M / cross.max()
               g *= max(scale, 0.0?) # might reduce S
               S = g.sum()
           if S>1+1e-4:
              return g/S
    # fallback simple
    row_max = convolve_fast(p_lp, np.ones(n)).max()? row sums of p: p convolved ones.
    if row_max>0:
       alpha = M / row_max
       g = np.full(n, alpha)
       S = g.sum()
       if S>1+1e-6: return g/S
    return None
```
Convolve_fast for two different arrays p,g needed for feasibility. We can implement general convolution: if n small direct `np.convolve`; else FFT. For LP fallback row_max, `np.convolve(p, np.ones(n))` direct O(n^2) but n small; can compute sliding window sum faster: row sum for k = sum of p over i with 0≤i,k-i<n = sum p[max(0,k-n+1):min(n,k+1)]. This is convolution with ones; compute via cumulative sum O(n). Let's implement `row_sums(p)`:
```
cum=np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(p)))
rows = np.empty(2n-1)
for k: i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)+1; rows[k]=cum[i1]-cum[i0]
```

Now, gradient L2/softmax. Do we need? LP may be enough if it works. But if LP fails due to solver unavailable, we need fallback local moves. We can implement a simple "soft gradient" improvement for starts. Let's design lightweight gradient step:
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    z = beta*c
    z = z - z.max()
    ex = np.exp(z)
    w = ex/ex.sum()
    return w

def grad_max(p, weights):
    n=p.size
    # H[i,j] = weights[i+j]
    idx = np.arange(n)[:,None] + np.arange(n)[None,:]
    H = weights[idx]
    grad = 2.0 * (H @ p)
    # Note derivative of weighted sum of c_k wrt p_i = 2∑_j weights[i+j] p_j (as above)
    return grad

def gradient_step(p, beta, lr):
    c = convolve(p,p)
    w = softmax_weights(c,beta)
    g = grad_max(p,w)
    # normalize g by c.sum? Step in a-space with simplex projection.
    q = p - lr*g
    q = np.clip(q,0,None)
    q /= q.sum()
    return q
```
This approximates descent on weighted sum of coefficients. Since objective max, choose beta. We can also use top active uniform: w=1 for top q, else0, normalized. Then grad. The gradient wrt logarithms? In a-space with projection, okay.
We can take line search t. Maybe easier: active uniform weights yields steepest descent for exact max with small step. Generating top q w (e.g. q=max(1,n//8)). This is cheap. The derivative of F at active coefficients uniform: grad_i=2∑_{active}(p_{k-i}) = 2 H @ p. Then update p ← simplex projection of p - α grad. This can reduce max. We can use this if LP fails. It doesn't require H full if n small; can compute H active by adding shifted p for each active k: For each active k, grad += p shifted? We can do loop over active q and add vector, O(q n), maybe q~50, n=500 ->25k per step, fine. Better than O(n^2) for sparse active.
```
def active_gradient(p, c, q=32):
    active = np.argsort(c)[-q:]
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    for k in active:
        i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
        # derivative for k: 2 p[k-i] for i in range, but if k-2i?? Wait derivative wc c_k = 2 p[k-i] for all i valid (ordered double count already included). Add 2*p[k-i].
        grad[i0:i1+1] += 2.0 * p[k - np.arange(i0,i1+1)]
    return grad/q
```

Let's design a generic `improve_with_lp(p)`:
```
def improve_with_lp(p, deadline, min_improve=1e-6):
    a = p.copy(); sc = score_fast(a); M = sc/(2*n)
    best = a; best_sc=sc
    while time < deadline:
       dir = lp_direction(a, M, time_limit=... )
       if dir is None:
           # try uniform direction if possible
           rowmax = row_sums(a).max()
           alpha = M/rowmax
           if n*alpha > 1+1e-4: dir = np.full(n, alpha)/(n*alpha)
           else: break
       # line search mixtures
       current=a; cur_sc=best_sc
       improved=False
       # h=dir
       test_ms = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7]
       for t in test_ms:
          cand=(1-t)*current + t*dir
          cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum()
          if s<1e-12: continue
          cand/=s
          sc=score_fast(cand)
          if sc < cur_sc - 1e-10:
              current=cand; cur_sc=sc; improved=True
       if not improved:
          # try smaller or mix 0.5 current with dir? Actually t small
          for t in [0.02,0.01,0.005]: ...
       if improved:
          a=current; best=a; best_sc=cur_sc; M=sc/(2*n); print
       else:
          # maybe gradient active step
          cand = active_step(a, c?); if improves, continue else break
    return best,best_sc
```
Need compute score_fast for line search; for LP M use exact/fast. Since LP direction used M from current best sc. If score_fast approximate, M maybe slightly off; feasibility check via cross. We can use exact for current before LP if size≤1000? But for speed use FFT. The LP constraint uses M approximate; if M too small by tolerance, LP infeasible. Add safety M_lp = M * 1.001. After solving, scale g to satisfy cross ≤M (actual). If M too small, scaling reduces S. But if M approximate exact close, okay. Use `M = max(score_fast(a)/(2*n), 1e-12)`.

Line search can use exact score for candidate if improve candidate near best, but fast okay. At end exact.

Active gradient step fallback:
```
def active_step(a, c, frac=0.05):
   n=a.size
   q=max(1,min(n, c.size//8))
   idx=np.argsort(c)[-q:]
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for k in idx:
       i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
       js=np.arange(i0,i1+1)
       g[js] += a[k-js]
   # derivative factor 2/q, step small
   t = 0.05

```
def self_convolve(a):
    n=a.size
    if n <= 1500:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       m = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
       fa = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
       fa *= fa
       c = np.fft.irfft(fa, m)[:2*n-1]
       # Clean negative tiny
       c = np.where(np.abs(c) < 1e-12, 0.0, c)
       return c
```
If using FFT on nonnegative, small negative could make max? Negative not issue for max; but exact score maybe overestimates if negative round. Clip all c negative to 0? Since true coeff nonnegative. `c = np.maximum(c,0)` but FFT round can produce tiny positive? Good.
- `score_direct(a)`: c=self_convolve; return 2*n*c.max() + maybe finite checks.
- `evaluate_best(seq)`: call global evaluate_sequence if available and finite, else direct? Use exact.

    # power [0.5,1,1.5,2,3]

def greedy_improve(a, c, score, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    n=a.size
    active_frac = max(1, int(n//40)) maybe 128 cap?
    no_improve=0
    accept_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # if c not finite or mismatch occasionally, recompute
        # compute T
        # two-pass: top c weights by softmax beta=20? Let's use active weights:
        # weights w_k = c_k for top Q? Normalize; maybe include small base for all top to have sum1.
        Q = max(5, min(256, n//4)) ??? For n=512 Q=128 maybe too many; T cost 65k per iter. Q= min(64, max(8,n//16)). Let's choose Q = min(64, max(8, n//8))? n=512 ->64; n=1024->64; n=2535->64. Good.
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -Q)[-Q:] if Q<c.size else np.arange(c.size)
        w = c[idx]
        wsum=w.sum(); if wsum<=0: w=np.ones_like(w)
        w=w/wsum
        T=np.zeros(n)
        for k, wk in zip(idx,w):
            i0=max(0,k-n+1); i1=min(n-1,k)
            if i0<=i1:
                T[i0:i1+1] += wk * a[k - np.arange(i0,i1+1)]
        # top sources and bottom targets
        if T.max()-T.min() <=1e-12: no_improve++; maybe break or random transfer
        # top_count=5, bot_count=5
        top_src = np.argpartition(T, -top_count)[-top_count:]
        bot_tgt = np.argpartition(T, bot_count)[:bot_count]
        sorted by T descending/ascending
        improved=False
        # try source top in order, target bottom
        for i in top_src:
            if a[i] <= 1e-12: continue
            for j in bot_tgt:
                if i==j: continue
                # if T[j] >= T[i] - maybe still skip if not much
                if T[j] >= T[i] - 1e-10: continue
                # try fractions [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8,0.95] maybe smaller if p_i small? 
                for frac in (0.2,0.5,0.8,0.95,0.05):
                    eps=frac*a[i]
                    newc, m = apply_move_max(c,a,i,j,eps)
                    if 2*n*m < score - 1e-10:
                       a[i]-=eps; a[j]+=eps; c=newc; score=2*n*m; improved=True; accept_count+=1
                       break
                if improved: break
            if improved: break
        if improved:
            no_improve=0
            # recompute c occasionally
            if accept_count % 50 ==0:
                c=self_convolve(a); score=2*n*c.max()
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            # if stuck, try greedy with different weights (softmax all) or random pair
            if no_improve % 10 == 0:
                # random finite move: choose source high a, target low a, eps=0.5a_i, and if improves accept
                i = rng.choice(n, p=a) maybe; j = rng.choice(n)
                ...
            if no_improve > max(200, n//2): break
    return a,c,score
```
`apply_move_max` should return a new c only if we need for accepted. Current code computes newc for every eps. To avoid allocation for failed, we can first compute max via a temporary but still allocation. Fine.
```
def move_newc(c,a,i,j,eps):
    n=a.size
    newc=c.copy()
    scale=2*eps
    newc[i:i+n] -= scale*a
    newc[j:j+n] += scale*a
    e2=eps*eps
    if 2*i < newc.size: newc[2*i]+=e2
    if 2*j < newc.size: newc[2*j]+=e2
    if i != j: newc[i+j] -= 2*e2
    # due to floating, maybe tiny negative
    # return newc, newc.max()
```

Could overlap with target shift continuous but second updates separate okay.

If no improvement in greedy due to local optimum, LP can help. LP direction from current a can be used as a "direction" and line search. We can integrate LP with best sequence:
```
def lp_improve(a, score, deadline, rng):
    n=a.size
    if n > 1200: return a,score
    M=score/(2*n)
    while time left:
       dir = get_lp_dir(a, M*1.01)
       if dir is None:
          # fallback uniform direction if rowmax...
          break
       # line search with t and maybe mix
       current=a; cur=score
       for t in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5]:
          cand=(1-t)*current + t*dir
          cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
          sc=score_direct(cand)
          if sc<cur-1e-9:
             current=cand; cur=sc
       if cur<score - 1e-9: score=cur; a=current
       else:
          # try small t 0.01
          ...
          if no: break
    return a,score
```

Function `normalize_seq(arr)`:
```
def normalize_seq(arr):
    a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1e12)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-15:
        return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
    return a/s
```
If arr length zero? return []? But final should nonempty.

`self_convolve(a)`:
```
def self_convolve(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    if n <= 1500:
        return np.convolve(a,a)
    m=1
    need=2*n-1
    while m < need: m <<= 1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    fa *= fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa, m)[:need]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
If a contains zeros, fine.

`score_of(a)`:
```
def score_of(a):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    if a.size<1 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return np.inf
    c=self_convolve(a)
    sm=a.sum()
    if sm < 0.01: return np.inf # but normalized sum1
    return float(2*a.size*c.max()/(sm*sm))
```
Our search often normalized sum1, so faster `score_from_c` just 2n*max.
`exact_score(list)`: calls `evaluate_sequence` if in globals; else score_of. Need no import.
```
def exact_score(seq):
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
       return score_of(np.asarray(seq,float))
    return float(val)
```
`evaluate_sequence` assumed imported.

`generate_starts(n, rng)`:
Need deterministic. Use top-level no closure. Return list.
```
def generate_starts(n, rng):
    out=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    # exponential
    for alpha in [0.5,0.75,1.0,1.25,1.5,2.0,3.0]:
       w=np.exp(-alpha*x)
       out.append(normalize_seq(w))
    # integer powers
    for g in [0.4,0.7,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0]:
       w=(1.0-x)**g + 1e-6
       out.append(normalize_seq(w))
       # reverse? maybe (x)**g
       w2=x**g +1e-6
       out.append(normalize_seq(w2))
    # inverse powers
    for A in [1.0,2.0,4.0,8.0]:
       for g in [0.7,1.0,1.5,2.0]:
          w=1.0/((1.0+A*x)**g)
          out.append(normalize_seq(w))
    # Fourier modulated exponential base
    for _ in range(20):
       alpha=float(rng.uniform(0.5,2.0))
       base=np.exp(-alpha*x)
       modes=0
       w=np.ones_like(x)
       for freq in [1,2,3,4]:
           A_amp=float(rng.uniform(0.0,0.6))
           phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
           term=A_amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
           w = w*(1.0+term)? This may go negative. Better additive: w0=1+sum terms
       w0=1+ sum terms
       w=base*w0
       w=np.clip(w,1e-6,None)
       out.append(normalize_seq(w))
    # random smooth: random Fourier sum with zero mean plus 1
    for _ in range(15):
       w=np.ones_like(x)
       for freq in range(1,8):
          amp=float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0/(freq+1)))*rng.choice([1,-1])? Use sign in phase.
          phase=rng.uniform(0,2π)
          w += amp*np.sin(2πfreq x+phase)
       w=np.clip(w,1e-6,None)
       # Multiply by a random skew base
       alpha=rng.uniform(0.3,2.0)
       w *= np.exp(-alpha*x)
       out.append(normalize_seq(w))
    return out
```
Avoid duplicate sequences; but okay.

Run starts:
```
def get_initial_candidates(deadline, rng):
    cands=[]
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if parent is not None:
       try: p=np.asarray(parent,float); if p.ndim !=1: flat; p=clip; if len>0: normalize and consider
       except: pass
    lengths = [256, 512, 768, 1024, 1536]
    # To keep within time, if n large skip if deadline tight
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60: break
       starts=generate_starts(n,rng)
       for s in starts:
           if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
           c=self_convolve(s); sc=2*n*c.max()
           if np.isfinite(sc): cands.append((sc,s,c))
    # sort by score
    cands.sort(key=lambda item: item[0])
    return cands
```
No lambdas? Rule says don't use lambda functions. We can sort with `cands.sort(key=score_key)` where top-level function `def candidate_key(item): return item[0]`. Use top-level.

   if a.size > 5000: # downsample? maybe return first?

          trunc=a[:block*(a.size//block)]? simpler: a = a[:5000]? score changes. Better keep if manageable? 100k length convolve impossible. The provided length 2535 likely <=5000. If >5000, downsample by averaging to 5000.

Greedy improve function needs `rng`, verbose maybe.
```
def greedy_improve(a, c, score, deadline, rng, min_score=None):
    a=a.copy(); n=a.size
    accept=0; stuck=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if score < (min_score if min_score is not None else 0): break? We can stop if close to lower bound 1? but target.
        # compute T
        c.size = 2n-1
        Q = min(100, max(8, c.size//10))
        if Q >= c.size:
            idx=np.arange(c.size); w=np.full(c.size,1.0/c.size)
        else:
            idx=np.argpartition(c, -Q)[-Q:]
            w=c[idx]
            ws=w.sum()
            if ws <= 1e-300: w=np.ones_like(w)
            else: w=w/ws
        T=np.zeros(n)
        # We can optimize loop using for:
        for k, wk in zip(idx,w):
            i0 = k - (n - 1)
            if i0 < 0: i0 = 0
            i1 = k
            if i1 >= n: i1 = n - 1
            if i0 <= i1:
                # create array p = a[k - np.arange(i0,i1+1)]
                T[i0:i1+1] += wk * a[k - np.arange(i0,i1+1)]
        # if no variation
        rangeT=T.max()-T.min()
        if rangeT <= 1e-14:
            stuck += 1
        else:
            stuck=0
        # choose candidates
        topC=6; botC=6
        if n < topC: top_idx=np.argsort(T)[::-1]; bot_idx=np.argsort(T)[:botC]
        else:
            top_idx=np.argpartition(T, -topC)[-topC:]; top_idx=top_idx[np.argsort(T[top_idx])[::-1]]
            bot_idx=np.argpartition(T, botC)[:botC]; bot_idx=bot_idx[np.argsort(T[bot_idx])]
        improved=False
        # try current pair combinations
        for i in top_idx:
            i=int(i); pi=float(a[i])
            if pi <= 1e-12: continue
            Ti=float(T[i])
            for j in bot_idx:
                j=int(j)
                if i==j: continue
                Tj=float(T[j])
                if Tj >= Ti - 1e-12: continue
                # if target already high maybe less effective, but try
                # fractions: start moderate
                for frac in (0.3,0.6,0.85,0.1,0.95,0.5):
                    eps=frac*pi
                    newc, nm = apply_move_and_max(c,a,i,j,eps)
                    ns=2*n*nm
                    if ns < score - 1e-10:
                        a[i]=pi-eps; a[j]=float(a[j])+eps
                        c=newc; score=ns; improved=True; accept+=1
                        break
                if improved: break
            if improved: break
        if improved:
            if accept % 100 == 0:
                c2=self_convolve(a)
                s2=2*n*c2.max()
                if np.isfinite(s2): c=s2; score=s2
            stuck=0
        else:
            stuck +=1
            if stuck >= max(200, n//2): break
            if stuck % 25 == 0:
                # random escape: choose source high, target low, large eps; try if improves
                i = int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
                if i!=j and a[i]>1e-9:
                    for frac in (0.5,0.8,0.95,0.2):
                       eps=frac*a[i]
                       newc,nm=apply_move_and_max(c,a,i,j,eps)
                       if 2*n*nm < score -1e-10:
                           a[i]-=eps; a[j]+=eps; c=newc; score=2*n*nm; broken; stuck=0
       if min_score is not None and score < min_score: break
    return a,c,score
```
Need top-level `apply_move_and_max` returns newc,max.
```
def apply_move_and_max(c, a, i, j, eps):
    n=a.size
    newc=c.copy()
    scale=2.0*eps
    newc[i:i+n] -= scale*a
    newc[j:j+n] += scale*a
    e2=eps*eps
    if 2*i < newc.size: newc[2*i] += e2
    if 2*j < newc.size: newc[2*j] += e2
    if i != j: newc[i+j] -= 2.0*e2
    # Due to floating, max may be tiny negative? no.
    m = float(newc.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m): return c, np.inf
    return newc, m
```
Important: `newc[i:i+n]` length n? Since c length 2n-1, slice i:i+n length n-i? Wait from i to i+n inclusive? Example n=6, i=5, slice 5:11 length6 on length11? c indices 0..10, slice 5:11 gives 5..10 length6 yes. Good. j same. p vector a length6. The valid rows k=i+j? for source i: k ranges i..i+n-1, length n, values p_0..p_{n-1}. Yes.
But when j > i? Ranges overlap; operations in-place: subtract source p then add target p. Since linear updates independent, order okay. However second-order cross update at i+j could be in range where linear updated; order okay after linear. Good.
But using `a` old; in the in-place newc, `scale*a` uses old a; okay.

One nuance: If source and target shifts overlap, the linear update should use old p for both; we do. The second-order cross at i+j should be -2e2, but what if 2i or 2j equals i+j (only if i=j). We handle i!=j.

`np.argpartition` with weight? If Q >= c.size use all.

Stuck random: If random improves, set improved? We'll just return eventually.

LP functions:
```
def row_sums_conv(p):
    n=p.size
    cum=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(p)))
    out=np.empty(2*n-1)
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       i0=k-n+1; if i0<0:i0=0
       i1=k+1; if i1>n:i1=n
       out[k]=cum[i1]-cum[i0]
    return out
```
Could use sliding window via `np.convolve(p, np.ones(n))` for small n; loop O(n) is fine.

`lp_direction(p, M, time_limit=20.0)`:
```
def lp_direction(p, M, time_limit=20.0):
    n=p.size
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_LINPROG or n > 900:
       # fallback simple
    p_lp = p + 1e-12
    p_lp = np.maximum(p_lp,0)
    # scale p_lp? sum1.
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    # fill: for i in range(n): A[i:i+n, i] = p_lp
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i+n, i] = p_lp
    cobj = -np.ones(n)
    b = M * np.ones(2*n-1)
    try:
       t0=time.monotonic()
       res=linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n, method='highs',
                   options={'presolve': True, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    except Exception:
       res=None
    if res is not None and res.success and res.x is not None:
       g=np.maximum(res.x,0.0)
       S=float(g.sum())
       if S>1.0+1e-6:
          # check feasibility
          cross = self_convolve_2(p_lp, g) # define
          cm=cross.max()
          if cm > M:
             if cm >0: g *= M/cm
             S=float(g.sum())
          if S>1.0+1e-5:
             return g/S
       # maybe still success but S<=1
    # fallback uniform
    rows=row_sums_conv(p_lp)
    rm=float(rows.max())
    if rm>0:
       alpha=M/rm
       S=n*alpha
       if S>1.0+1e-6:
          g=np.full(n, alpha)
          return g/S
    return None
```

def lp_improve(a, score, deadline, rng):
    n=a.size

    a=a.copy()
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       M=max(score/(2*n),1e-15)
       dir = lp_direction(a, M*1.02, time_limit=max(5.0, min(30.0, deadline - time.monotonic() -60)))
       if dir is None: break
       current=a; cur=score; im=False
       ts = [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0]
       for t in ts:
          cand = (1.0-t)*a + t*dir  # use current a? should use current best from previous t? We'll test independent from original a
If a loses due to numerical, use original for each t to avoid moving worse then trying further.
          cand=normalize_seq(cand)
          sc=score_of(cand)
          if sc < cur - 1e-9:
             cur=sc; current=cand; im=True
       if im:
          a=current; score=cur; it+=1
       else:
          # try mix with p and dir 0.5? If dir alone bad, maybe small t already. Break.
          break
    return a,score

def improve_candidate(a, c, score, deadline, rng):
    n=a.size
    a=a.copy()
    # Ensure c matches score and length. If not, recompute.
    if c is None or len(c)!=2*n-1 or abs(score - 2*n*c.max())>1e-6:
       c=self_convolve(a); score=2*n*c.max()
    # Greedy first: if n large or time, maybe 50%
    t1 = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 0.55*(deadline-time.monotonic()))? Simpler: 
    if n <= 2000:
       greedy_dead = time.monotonic() + min( dead assum, max(20, 0.6*(deadline-time.monotonic())))
    else:
       greedy_dead = deadline - 5
    a,c,sc = greedy_improve(a,c,sc, greedy_dead, rng)
    # LP if n<=800
    if n <= 800 and HAVE_SCIPY_LINPROG and time.monotonic() < deadline - 45:
       lp_dead = time.monotonic() + min(120.0, 0.3*(deadline-time.monotonic()))
       a,sc2 = lp_improve(a, sc, lp_dead, rng)
       if sc2 < sc: sc=sc2; c=self_convolve(a)
    # second greedy until deadline
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       a,c,sc = greedy_improve(a,c,sc, deadline-5, rng)
    return a,c,sc

Let's refine `generate_starts` to avoid too many near duplicate and ensure nonnegative.
```
def generate_starts(n, rng):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out=[]
    def add(w): w=np.asarray(w,float); if w.size==n: w=np.clip(w,1e-8, None); add normalized if sum finite
    # Exponential
    for alpha in [0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0,1.3,1.7,2.2,3.0]: add(np.exp(-alpha*x))
    # Power right/left
    for a in [0.4,0.7,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0]:
        add(x**a + 1e-6)
        add((1.0-x)**a + 1e-6)
    # inverse
    for A in [1.0,2.0,4.0,8.0]:
        for b in [0.7,1.0,1.5,2.0]: add(1.0/((1.0+A*x)**b))
    # Fourier/base
    for _ in range(24):
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 2.5))
        base = np.exp(-alpha*x)
        w0 = np.ones(n)
        for freq in [1,2,3,4]:
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.7 * (1.0/freq)))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2*math.pi))
            w0 += amp*np.sin(2*math.pi*freq*x + phase)
        w = base * np.clip(w0, 0.05, None)
        add(w)
    # random random with skew
    for _ in range(16):
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.3,2.5))
        base=np.exp(-alpha*x)
        w=np.ones(n)
        for freq in range(1,10):
            amp=float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0/freq))
            phase=float(rng.uniform(0.0,2*math.pi))
            w += amp*np.sin(2*math.pi*freq*x+phase)
        w=base*np.clip(w,0.02,None)
        add(w)
    # remove duplicates? optional
    return out
```
`add` nested not allowed! Must top-level? Rule: all helper functions top-level and no closures from nesting. So cannot define `add` inside generate_starts unless top-level? We can either not use helper or define top-level `try_add_start` but it needs out; can use top-level `normalize_and_check(w)` returning arr or None. In generate, loop `w=...; arr=normalize_seq(w); if arr is not None: out.append(arr)`.

Normalize_seq returns None if invalid? We'll make it return array or None:
```
def normalize_seq(arr, min_len=1):
   a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
   if a.size<min_len: return None
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1e12)
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-15: return None
   return a/s
```

   if p is None: return None
   try: a=np.asarray(p.ravel(), dtype=np.float64)
   except: return None
   if a.size ==0: return None
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return None
   return a/s

`self_convolve_2`: Use threshold product. For LP cross with p length n and g length n, product n^2; threshold maybe 2e6. If >, FFT. Need right length need=2n-1, power of two.
```
def convolve2(a,b):
    la,lb=a.size,b.size
    if la*lb <= 4_000_000:
       return np.convolve(a,b)
    m=1; need=la+lb-1; while m<need: m<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,m); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,m)[:need]
    return np.maximum(c,0.0)
```
Good.

def resample_blocks(a,target):
   n=a.size; if target<=0 or n<target: return None
   start = np.floor(np.linspace(0,n,target+1)).astype(int)
   out=np.zeros(target)
   for i in range(target):
      s=start[i]; e=start[i+1]
      if e<=s: out[i]=a[s]
      else: out[i]=a[s:e].mean()

Top-level `block_perturb(a, rng)`:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng):
  n=a.size; cand=a.copy()
  width=max(1,int(n*rng.uniform(0.02,0.2)))
  start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
  factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.35))
  cand[start:start+width]*=factor
  if rng.random()<0.3: cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
  return normalize_seq(cand)
```
But reversing may symmetrize and score≥2; maybe avoid symmetric. We can just perturb and maybe random spike. Use as additional candidates.

def block_perturb(a,rng):
   a=normalize_seq(a)
   if a is None: return None
   n=a.size; cand=a.copy()
   width=max(1,int(n*rng.uniform(0.01,0.25)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
   factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.4)))
   cand[start:start+width]*=factor
   # optional random sparse shift
   if rng.random()<0.3:
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n)); eps=0.2*cand[i]; cand[i]-=eps; cand[j]+=eps
   return normalize_seq(cand)
```

def improve_candidate(a, c, score, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_seq(a)
    if a is None: return None,None,np.inf
    n=a.size
    if c is None or len(c)!=2*n-1:
       c=self_convolve(a); score=2*n*c.max()
    else:
       score=2*n*c.max()  # trust c
    # if score not finite recompute
    if not np.isfinite(score):
       c=self_convolve(a); score=2*n*c.max()
    if n > 6000: return a,c,score
    # stage1 greedy: 60% of remaining
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()

    greedy1_dead = time.monotonic() + min(rem, max(10.0, 0.55*rem))

def lp_improve(a, score, deadline, rng):
   a=normalize_seq(a)
   if a is None: return None,np.inf
   n=a.size
   if n > 800 or not HAVE_SCIPY_LINPROG: return a.copy(), score
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      M=max(float(score)/(2.0*n), 1e-15)
      dir=lp_direction(a, M*1.02, time_limit= max(5.0, min(30.0, deadline-time.monotonic()-10)))
      if dir is None: break
      best=a.copy(); best_sc=score
      ts=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0)
      for t in ts:
          cand=(1.0-t)*a + t*dir
          cand=normalize_seq(cand)
          if cand is None: continue
          sc=score_normalized(cand)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc -1e-10:
             best=cand; best_sc=sc
      if best_sc < score -1e-10:
          a=best; score=best_sc
      else:
          break
   return a, score
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a)^2)

Strategy:
- Start from many asymmetric smooth 1-D priors (exponential/ramped/Fourier-modulated)
  and from the provided height_sequence_1 when available.
- Improve sequences with:
  * greedy coordinate transfers guided by active maximum convolution coefficients
  * a linear-programming direction that maximizes sum(g) subject to a*g <= M
  * short line-searches toward the improved directions
- Keep the best exact-evaluated candidate and return it before the deadline.
"""

import math
import time

import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_LP = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_LP = False


def candidate_score_key(item):
    return item[0]


def add_candidate(candidates, a, c, sc):
    if a is None or c is None:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or a.size == 0:
        return
    candidates.append((float(sc), np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy(),
                       np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()))


def add_pool(pool, a, max_len=8000, max_items=8):
    if pool is None or a is None:
        return
    b = normalize_seq(a)
    if b is None or b.size > max_len or len(pool) >= max_items:
        return
    pool.append(b.copy())


def normalize_seq(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1e12)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s


def self_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1500:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1
    while m < need:
        m <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    fa *= fa
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa, m)[:need]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def convolve2(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    la = int(a.size)
    lb = int(b.size)
    if la == 0 or lb == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if la * lb <= 4_000_000:
        return np.convolve(a, b)
    need = la + lb - 1
    m = 1
    while m < need:
        m <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, m)[:need]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def score_normalized(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = self_convolve(a)
    return float(2.0 * a.size * c.max())


def sync_sequence(a):
    b = normalize_seq(a)
    if b is None:
        return None, None, np.inf
    c = self_convolve(b)
    return b, c, float(2.0 * b.size * c.max())


def exact_score(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])
    except Exception:
        a = normalize_seq(a)
        if a is None:
            return np.inf
        c = self_convolve(a)
        return float(2.0 * a.size * c.max())
    return float(val)


def apply_move_and_max(c, a, i, j, eps):
    i = int(i)
    j = int(j)
    eps = float(eps)
    if eps <= 0.0:
        return c, float(c.max())
    n = int(a.size)
    newc = c.copy()
    scale = 2.0 * eps
    newc[i:i + n] -= scale * a
    newc[j:j + n] += scale * a

    e2 = eps * eps
    if 2 * i < newc.size:
        newc[2 * i] += e2
    if 2 * j < newc.size:
        newc[2 * j] += e2
    if i != j:
        newc[i + j] -= 2.0 * e2

    m = float(newc.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return c, np.inf
    return newc, m


def greedy_improve(a, c, score, deadline, rng):
    a, c, score = sync_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, None, np.inf

    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 2:
        return a, c, score

    accept = 0
    stuck = 0
    best_local_score = score

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if n < 2:
            break

        m = len(c)
        Q = min(64, max(8, m // 16))
        if Q >= m:
            idx = np.arange(m)
            w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, m)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -Q)[-Q:]
            w = c[idx].astype(np.float64)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws <= 1e-300:
                w = np.ones_like(w) / float(w.size)
            else:
                w = w / ws

        T = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k, wk in zip(idx, w):
            k = int(k)
            i0 = k - (n - 1)
            if i0 < 0:
                i0 = 0
            i1 = k
            if i1 >= n:
                i1 = n - 1
            if i0 <= i1:
                T[i0:i1 + 1] += wk * a[k - np.arange(i0, i1 + 1)]

        rmax = float(T.max())
        rmin = float(T.min())
        if not np.isfinite(rmax) or (rmax - rmin) <= 1e-14:
            stuck += 1
        else:
            stuck = 0

        topC = min(8, n)
        botC = min(8, n)

        if n <= topC:
            top_idx = np.argsort(T)[::-1]
        else:
            top_idx = np.argpartition(T, -topC)[-topC:]
            top_idx = top_idx[np.argsort(T[top_idx])[::-1]]

        if n <= botC:
            bot_idx = np.argsort(T)
        else:
            bot_idx = np.argpartition(T, botC)[:botC]
            bot_idx = bot_idx[np.argsort(T[bot_idx])]

        improved = False
        fracs = (0.35, 0.6, 0.85, 0.15, 0.95, 0.5, 0.2)

        for ii in top_idx:
            i = int(ii)
            pi = float(a[i])
            if pi <= 1e-12:
                continue
            Ti = float(T[i])
            for jj in bot_idx:
                j = int(jj)
                if i == j:
                    continue
                Tj = float(T[j])
                if Tj >= Ti - 1e-12:
                    continue
                for frac in fracs:
                    eps = frac * pi
                    if eps <= 0:
                        continue
                    newc, nm = apply_move_and_max(c, a, i, j, eps)
                    ns = 2.0 * n * nm
                    if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < score - 1e-10:
                        a[i] = pi - eps
                        a[j] = float(a[j]) + eps
                        c = newc
                        score = ns
                        best_local_score = min(best_local_score, score)
                        improved = True
                        accept += 1
                        break
                if improved:
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if improved:
            stuck = 0
            if accept % 80 == 0:
                a, c, score = sync_sequence(a)
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck > 220:
                break

            if stuck % 25 == 0:
                # occasional random transfer to escape shallow local minima
                for _ in range(6):
                    i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                    j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                    if i == j or float(a[i]) <= 1e-12:
                        continue
                    pi = float(a[i])
                    for frac in (0.8, 0.5, 0.95, 0.3, 0.9):
                        eps = frac * pi
                        newc, nm = apply_move_and_max(c, a, i, j, eps)
                        ns = 2.0 * n * nm
                        if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < score - 1e-10:
                            a[i] = pi - eps
                            a[j] = float(a[j]) + eps
                            c = newc
                            score = ns
                            best_local_score = min(best_local_score, score)
                            stuck = 0
                            break
                    if stuck == 0:
                        break

    return a, c, score


def row_sums_conv(p):
    n = int(p.size)
    cum = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(p, dtype=np.float64)))
    out = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(2 * n - 1):
        i0 = k - n + 1
        if i0 < 0:
            i0 = 0
        i1 = k + 1
        if i1 > n:
            i1 = n
        out[k] = cum[i1] - cum[i0]
    return out


def uniform_direction(p, M):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None
    rows = row_sums_conv(p)
    rm = float(rows.max())
    if rm <= 0:
        return None
    alpha = float(M) / rm
    S = n * alpha
    if S > 1.0 + 1e-6:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return None


def lp_direction(p, M, time_limit=30.0):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None

    p_lp = p + 1e-12
    p_lp = np.maximum(p_lp, 0.0)

    if HAVE_LP and n <= 800:
        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(n):
            A[i:i + n, i] = p_lp

        cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

        res = None
        options = {
            "presolve": True,
            "primal_feasibility_tolerance": 1e-9,
            "dual_feasibility_tolerance": 1e-9,
            "time_limit": float(max(1.0, time_limit)),
        }
        try:
            res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds,
                          method="highs", options=options)
        except Exception:
            res = None

        if res is not None and getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
            g = np.maximum(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
            S = float(g.sum())
            if S > 1.0 + 1e-6:
                cap = 100.0 / n
                if g.max() > cap:
                    g = np.minimum(g, cap)
                    S = float(g.sum())
                if S > 1.0 + 1e-5:
                    cross = convolve2(p_lp, g)
                    cm = float(cross.max())
                    if cm > float(M) and cm > 0:
                        g *= float(M) / cm
                        S = float(g.sum())
                    if S > 1.0 + 1e-5:
                        h = g / S
                        return np.maximum(h, 0.0)

    return uniform_direction(p_lp, float(M))


def lp_improve(a, score, deadline, rng):
    a, c, score = sync_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, np.inf

    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 2 or n > 800 or not HAVE_LP:
        return a.copy(), score

    while time.monotonic() < max(0.0, deadline):
        M = max(float(score) / (2.0 * n), 1e-15)
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 30.0:
            break
        tl = max(5.0, min(35.0, rem - 15.0))
        d = lp_direction(a, M * 1.02, time_limit=tl)
        if d is None:
            break

        best = a.copy()
        best_sc = score
        for t in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.55, 0.75, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * d
            cand = normalize_seq(cand)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            sc = score_normalized(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-10:
                best = cand
                best_sc = sc

        if best_sc < score - 1e-10:
            a, c, score = sync_sequence(best)
        else:
            break

    return a, score


def generate_starts(n, rng):
    out = []
    if n < 16:
        return out

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    two_pi = 2.0 * math.pi

    def try_add(w):
        b = normalize_seq(w)
        if b is not None and b.size == n:
            out.append(b)

    for alpha in (0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.0, 1.3, 1.7, 2.2, 3.0):
        try_add(np.exp(-alpha * x))

    for g in (0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        try_add(x ** g + 1e-6)
        try_add((1.0 - x) ** g + 1e-6)

    for A in (1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        for b in (0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0):
            try_add(1.0 / ((1.0 + A * x) ** b))

    for _ in range(24):
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 2.5))
        base = np.exp(-alpha * x)
        w0 = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for freq in (1, 2, 3, 4):
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.7 / freq))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, two_pi))
            w0 = w0 + amp * np.sin(two_pi * freq * x + phase)
        try_add(base * np.clip(w0, 0.03, None))

    for _ in range(16):
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 2.5))
        base = np.exp(-alpha * x)
        w = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for freq in range(1, 10):
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0 / freq))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, two_pi))
            w = w + amp * np.sin(two_pi * freq * x + phase)
        try_add(base * np.clip(w, 0.02, None))

    for _ in range(12):
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.5, 2.5))
        center = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))
        w = np.exp(-alpha * x)
        if sigma > 0:
            w = w + 0.8 * np.exp(-((x - center) / sigma) ** 2)
        try_add(w)

    return out


def get_parent_seq():
    p = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if p is None:
        return None
    try:
        a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    return normalize_seq(a)


def resample_blocks(a, target):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    target = int(target)
    if n < target or target <= 0:
        return None
    starts = np.floor(np.linspace(0, n, target + 1)).astype(int)
    out = np.zeros(target, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(target):
        s = int(starts[i])
        e = int(starts[i + 1])
        if e <= s:
            out[i] = a[s]
        else:
            out[i] = float(a[s:e].mean())
    return normalize_seq(out)


def block_perturb(a, rng):
    a = normalize_seq(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    n = int(a.size)
    cand = a.copy()
    width = max(1, int(n * rng.uniform(0.01, 0.25)))
    width = min(width, n - 1)
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
    factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.45)))
    cand[start:start + width] *= factor

    if rng.random() < 0.45:
        i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        if i != j and float(cand[i]) > 1e-12:
            eps = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.5)) * float(cand[i])
            cand[i] -= eps
            cand[j] += eps

    return normalize_seq(cand)


def improve_candidate(a, c, score, deadline, rng):
    a, c, score = sync_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, None, np.inf
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or n > 8000:
        return a, c, score

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem <= 5:
        return a, c, score

    g1 = time.monotonic() + min(rem, max(10.0, 0.55 * rem))
    a, c, score = greedy_improve(a, c, score, g1, rng)
    if a is None:
        return None, None, np.inf

    if n <= 800 and HAVE_LP:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 60:
            lp_dead = time.monotonic() + min(180.0, 0.4 * rem)
            a2, sc2 = lp_improve(a, score, lp_dead, rng)
            if sc2 < score - 1e-12:
                a, c, score = sync_sequence(a2)
                if a is None:
                    return None, None, np.inf

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
        a, c, score = greedy_improve(a, c, score, deadline - 5, rng)

    return a, c, score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Main entry point: searches for a high-quality coefficient sequence."""
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0:
        budget = 1800.0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(30.0, budget - 20.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    candidates = []
    final_pool = []

    # Use the provided previous construction if possible.
    parent = get_parent_seq()
    if parent is not None and 0 < parent.size <= 8000:
        cpar = self_convolve(parent)
        spar = float(2.0 * parent.size * cpar.max())
        if np.isfinite(spar):
            add_candidate(candidates, parent, cpar, spar)
            add_pool(final_pool, parent)
            print(f"initial parent length={parent.size} score={spar:.9f}", flush=True)

        if parent.size > 1200:
            for target in (512, 1024):
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20:
                    break
                down = resample_blocks(parent, target)
                if down is not None:
                    cd = self_convolve(down)
                    sd = float(2.0 * down.size * cd.max())
                    if np.isfinite(sd):
                        add_candidate(candidates, down, cd, sd)
                        add_pool(final_pool, down)
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10:
                    break

    # Generate random/asymmetric starts at several lengths.
    lengths = (256, 512, 768, 1024)
    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30:
            break
        starts = generate_starts(n, rng)
        for s in starts:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5:
                break
            c = self_convolve(s)
            sc = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(candidates, s, c, sc)

    # Create a few perturbations of the best initial few.
    if candidates:
        candidates.sort(key=candidate_score_key)
        for sc, a, c in candidates[:2]:
            for _ in range(3):
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5:
                    break
                p = block_perturb(a, rng)
                if p is not None:
                    cp = self_convolve(p)
                    sp = float(2.0 * p.size * cp.max())
                    if np.isfinite(sp):
                        add_candidate(candidates, p, cp, sp)
        candidates.sort(key=candidate_score_key)

    if not candidates:
        a = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        c = self_convolve(a)
        sc = float(2.0 * a.size * c.max())
        add_candidate(candidates, a, c, sc)
        candidates.sort(key=candidate_score_key)

    best_seq, best_c, best_score = None, None, np.inf
    for sc, a, c in candidates:
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq, best_c, best_score = a.copy(), c.copy(), float(sc)
    if best_seq is not None:
        add_pool(final_pool, best_seq)

    # Improve the top few candidates.
    top = []
    seen_lengths = set()
    for item in candidates:
        sc, a, c = item
        if a.size in seen_lengths:
            continue
        seen_lengths.add(a.size)
        top.append(item)
        if len(top) >= 4:
            break

    remaining_initial = deadline - time.monotonic()
    for pos, (sc0, a0, c0) in enumerate(top):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 35:
            break

        slot = remaining / float(max(1, len(top) - pos))
        slot = min(slot, 900.0)
        slot = max(25.0, slot)
        local_deadline = time.monotonic() + min(slot, max(5.0, remaining - 10.0))

        a1, c1, sc1 = improve_candidate(a0.copy(), c0.copy(), sc0, local_deadline, rng)
        if a1 is not None and np.isfinite(sc1):
            if sc1 < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_seq, best_c, best_score = a1.copy(), c1.copy(), float(sc1)
            add_pool(final_pool, a1)
            print(f"improved candidate len={a1.size} score={sc1:.9f} "
                  f"best={best_score:.9f} time_left={deadline - time.monotonic():.1f}",
                  flush=True)

    # Final polishing on global best.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15:
        a_final, c_final, sc_final = sync_sequence(best_seq)
        a_final, c_final, sc_final = improve_candidate(
            a_final, c_final, sc_final, deadline - 5, rng
        )
        if a_final is not None and np.isfinite(sc_final):
            if sc_final < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_seq, best_c, best_score = a_final.copy(), c_final.copy(), float(sc_final)
            add_pool(final_pool, a_final)

    # Choose the best by exact evaluator when possible.
    chosen = None
    chosen_exact = np.inf

    for a in final_pool:
        a = normalize_seq(a)
        if a is None:
            continue
        try:
            es = exact_score(a)
        except Exception:
            es = score_normalized(a)
        if np.isfinite(es) and es < chosen_exact:
            chosen_exact = float(es)
            chosen = a.copy()

    if chosen is None and best_seq is not None:
        chosen = best_seq.copy()
        chosen_exact = best_score

    if chosen is None:
        chosen = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        chosen_exact = 2.0

    chosen = normalize_seq(chosen)
    if chosen is None:
        chosen = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0

    print(f"final exact search score (best exact in pool)={chosen_exact:.9f} "
          f"length={chosen.size} time_used={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}", flush=True)

    return [float(x) for x in chosen]
```
